¬

{baghlah}Baghlah

{baghlah_descr}
The Baghlah is a large ship that can weigh up to 400 tonnes. Enterprising captains can mount swivel cannons on her hull to ward off pirates, and larger opponents are wary to cross decks in an open fight. It can cross great distances effectively, making it invaluable for the safe passage of troops and supplies to distant shores.


{baghlah_descr_short}
Long distance sailing ship, with mounted swivel guns, useful for transporting troops and supplies.

¬----------------

{carrack}Carraca

{carrack_descr}
The Carrack is a large four-masted vessel that is capable of holding many men and supplies, able to outmatch most other competing ships. It is stable on heavy seas, and roomy enough to carry provisions for long voyages. They are well armed, sporting broadside firing cannon arrays as well as a large crew of competent onboard marines.

{carrack_descr_short}
A large capital ship. Capable of travelling across the ocean.

¬----------------

{dhow}Buque de guerra

{dhow_descr}
A warboat will transport armies and provide some minor protection against pirates. Against other ships of its kind, it may prevail, but it should not be expected to hold up against any ship of true quality.

{dhow_descr_short}
This simple boat will transport armies and provide some protection against pirates.

¬----------------

{dragon_boat}Buque dragón

{dragon_boat_descr}
Dragon Boats are the command ships of Skyrim's navy. They are equipped with a strong keel and a double set of hardy Nord rowers. These boats carry ballistae for firing grappling hooks to pull enemy ships into contact, enabling boarding actions. While not too cheap to produce, these are some of the safer ships to be on in a naval engagement.


{dragon_boat_descr_short}
A strong Nord warship, able to carry many men as well as grappling hooks for boarding actions.

¬----------------

{dromon}Dromon

{dromon_descr}
Though light and manoeuvrable, the Dromon is a ship of war and should not be underestimated. Boastful sailors would say this ship can outrun foes that it cannot defeat outright, though the validity of such a claim is up for debate.

{dromon_descr_short}
This light and manoeuvrable warship is a potent fighter, not to be underestimated.

¬----------------

{cog}Coca

{cog_descr}The Cog is equipped with a removable keel, steering oar, a single sail and a crew to see her by that carry bows to provide basic combat ability. Overall a general purpose light transport, capable of moving troops a fair distance while competing with other ships of its kind. However, it should not be trusted to endure in a serious engagement with a more advanced fleet.

{cog_descr_short}Small transport ship with a group of archers on board.

This ship is a greater advancement for those seeking a more sturdy fleet. It is capable transport and defeating less developed navies. Constructed of solid, durable oak, it can carry troops and defend itself much better than its smaller naval cousins. It is not the greatest ship for commanding the seas, but it is a good start.


{holk_descr_short}
Solid, reliable transport.

{w_holk}Holk mercenario

{w_holk_descr}
This mercenary ship is capable of holding its own in a naval battle, something invaluable to the captain that needs ships quickly. It is more expensive than a homegrown crew, but the smell of coin is an attractive and swift lure - perfect in an emergency. Constructed of solid oak and durable, it would be best used in support of a larger fleet or with short term tasks - but it can prove worth the money if put to good use.

{w_holk_descr_short}
Intermediate mercenary ship, useful in a fight or for short term tasks.

¬----------------

{caravel}Carabela

{caravel_descr}
A small three-masted ship, not particularly strong, but fast and capable. It is highly maneuverable and capable of withstanding a long journey on the high seas, and handling pirates or minor annoyances from hostile nations.

{caravel_descr_short}
A small three-masted vessel, ideally suited for long crossings.

¬----------------

{fire_ship}Barco de fuego

{fire_ship_descr}
The Fire Ship is a Aldmeri construct tweaked to carry a flamethrower used to spew out magic fire at unfortunate foes. The flamethrower consists of a copper tube and a set of bellows which can pump out fiery death on to enemy ships, burning both enemy crew and vessel, and occasionally the attackers as well.

{fire_ship_descr_short}
A Dromon modified to spit fiery death upon its enemies.

¬----------------

{galleass}Galeaza

{galleass_descr}
Galleasses are a respectable evolution of naval strength in Tamriel. Far larger than war galleys and much harder to board, Galleasses feature a forecastle resembling a miniature turret, with forward mounted centreline cannons as well as side facing cannons. Galleasses carry more sails than true galleys, and are far deadlier. A broadside from a Galleass can devastate most ships. A fearsome opponent, crafted for the comfort and pride of a seafaring lord.


{galleass_descr_short}
The strongest galley available to generals. With cannons facing all the way around the prow, it can devastate most opposition.

¬----------------

{galley}Galera

{galley_descr}
The basic Galley design is relatively inexpensive and useful for the price and time it takes to build. It is propelled by banks of oars and protected with a ram and marines for melee. The Galley has supplemental sails for getting to battles without tiring the men and are commonly featured (by some name or another) all across the seas.

{galley_descr_short}
Oar powered vessels equipped with a ram and fighting crew.

¬----------------

{grande_carrack}Carraca grande

{grande_carrack_descr}
The Grande Carrack is one of the greatest ships one can ever hope to see in Tamriel. Larger and better armed and armoured than virtually any other ship, this awesome example of sea power is a hard nut to crack.

{grande_carrack_descr_short}
A supremely powerful warship. Sporting heavy cannon broadsides and a thick hull. Can travel across the ocean. 

¬----------------

{gun_holk}Holk con cañones

{gun_holk_descr}
The Gun Holk is an upgrade of the basic Holk design. Constructed of tough oak, equipped with two sails and a greatly increased fore and aft castle armed with a battery of cannons. The soldiers on these vessels are equipped with gun powder weapons, making this a powerful ship, able to take on other ships and repel boarding actions.

{gun_holk_descr_short}
An upgraded Holk, armed with cannons in the fore castle and extra marines armed with gunpowder weapons.

¬----------------

{ladya}Lad'Ya

{ladya_descr}The Lad’ya is a Nordic ship akin to a Cog crossed with a Longboat. Equipped to carry soldiers and catapults as well, this ship is a deadly vessel against ill defended targets.

{ladya_descr_short}Carries soldiers and catapults for raiding land and smashing sea targets.

¬----------------

{lanternas}Lanternas

{lanternas_descr}
The Lanternas is the premier version of the war galley, equipped with gunpowder weaponry! Being larger, heavier, more durable and better equipped than the war galley, the Lanternas is invaluable to a naval commander. Featuring multiple centreline guns and some flanking guns, it can take on and destroy war galleys. 

{lanternas_descr_short}
A superior version of the war galley equipped with gunpowder weapons. Large, heavier and more durable than a war galley.

¬----------------

{longboat}Knorr

{longboat_descr}Knorr is one of the types of Nordic wooden ships. For the construction of knorr used pine and ash, less oak. In motion, the knorr is driven by oars or a direct rake sail and is controlled by a stern steering wheel.

{longboat_descr_short}Nordic warship capable of taking warriors aboard.

¬----------------

{war_galley}Galera de guerra

{war_galley_descr}
War Galleys are larger than normal galleys as a result of being equipped with a small assortment of siege equipment, better armor and more men. They also boast a larger compliment of oarsmen and soldiers onboard than lesser boats. The standard tactic for engaging is to disable the opposing ships' sails, then ram and board it. 

{war_galley_descr_short}
War Galleys are larger than normal galleys. Equipped with ballista or catapults.

¬----------------

{pirate_ship}Barco pirata

{pirate_ship_descr}
The Pirate ship is a scourge across Tamriel, harassing, raiding, murdering and slaving all they come across. A danger to underdeveloped ships, though a stronger fleet can easily shake them off.

{pirate_ship_descr_short}
A Pirate ship! Commanded by a traitorous Admiral seeking fame and fortune!

The Corsair Pirate ship is similar to a cog but used by fearless admirals who mostly seek fame and fortune for their own cause. Carries many fierce brigands armed with bows and short swords for assaulting enemy ships.

A Corsair ship commanded by a fearless Admiral seeking fame and fortune!

{Hlaalu_Nobles}Nobles hlaalu

{Hlaalu_Nobles_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain malus: Snow\n\nGreat House Hlaalu is currently the most influential off all the Great Houses. One of its own, Hlaalu Helseth, is by the grace of the Empire king of whole Morrowind. Despite the fact that Hlaalu are the youngest of Houses, they managed to completely monopolize external and internal trade lines, as well as securing their position at court. As House Hlaalu's territory borders that of Cyrodiil and though they are not a warrior house, the Hlaalu quickly embraced the Empire soon after they arrived in Morrowind. House Hlaalu welcomes Imperial Law and the Legions, and the freedom of trade and religion. Most Hlaalu are bribable, either with gold or with other favours, with their leaders as no exception. House Hlaalu retainers are also masters of thievery, sneaking, lock-picking, blackmailing and backstabbing, both literal and metaphorical. As soon as Hlaalu reached for lands of the other Houses, they clashed with them, and found themselves in need of creating more professional troops. Typical Armun-Ra Bonemold armor of native Hlaalu design is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Hlaalu design allows its wearer to simultaneously protect himself, and retain high flexibility of movement. Hlaalu aren't well known as kombatants, but their noblemen can fight side by side with mere cosdiers to encourage them in battle.

{Hlaalu_Nobles_descr_short}Noblemen in heavy armor with two-handed swords.

{Companions_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\n	The Companions are a proud, Skyrim based order of veteran fighters that have roots extending to the Five Hundred Companions of Isgramor. They are descendents of crewmen who created a large feast hall atop a hill that is now part of the city of Whiterun. Indeed, Whiterun's very existance can be credited to the founders of this order. They have no true leadership structure. Instead, they can be hired, individually or in crews such as these, to serve as honorable adventuring warriors that strengthen the backbone of any army. They are Skyrim's version of the southern Fighters Guild.

{Akavir_Pikemen}Syffim con naginatas

{Akavir_Pikemen_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 130%\nFree Upkeep Unit\n\nAfter the failed Akaviri invasion of Tamriel, many Tsaesci, unable to return home, were forced to integrate into local society. Many of their soldiers joined the Imperial Legion and brought about a lot of innovation based on their own military experience and training. Most scholars agree that the current structure, function, and make-up of the modern Imperial legions can be traced back to a Akaviri-influenced reform. Akaviri Tsaesci are clad in heavy armor and armed with dai-katanas; their cultural and military upbringing conditions them to abhor shields. One of the most famous Akaviri quotes--uttered by Pontetate Versidue-Shaie to Emperor Reman III--represents the common Akaviri view: "If you don't want to get hit, you move out of the way."

{Akavir_Pikemen_descr_short}Tsaesci soldiers, equipped with heavy armor and naginatas.

{Ashlanders}Jinetes cenicios independientes

{Ashlanders_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 110%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nSecondary effective against armour\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 50 meters\nMissiles: 6\nTerrain bonus: Snow\n\nAshlanders -- Dunmeri nomadic tribes with a strong sense of pride and tradition. They primarily live in the Ashlands, never staying in one spot for too long before moving onto the next. Each tribe is led by a Chieftain, but a great deal of respect is given to the shaman of the tribe who serves as a source of information and prophecy. Ashlanders adhere to ancient traditions and are opposed to the "cult of the Tribunal"; they remember that it was Nerevar who killed Dagoth Ur and was later betrayed by the Tribunal. The Raiders of the Ashlands are expert guerrilla warriors, hitting the enemies from close distance with deadly javelins before using their sharpened glass weapons in close fights. Although many Ashlanders will fight to the death to protect their tribe and beliefs; the Raiders of these tribes are usually less loyal and willing to serve as mercenaries, employing hit-and-run tactics and using their faithful mounts, the guar.

{Ashlanders_descr_short}Ashlanders expert in rapid raids and offering their service for money.

{Hist_Priest}Sacerdotes del hist

{Hist_Priest_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 110 meters\nMissiles: 5\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nGunpowder Unit\n\nThe Hist are sentient, spore-bearing trees native to Black Marsh that appear to be connected to each other--physically and mentally--through an elaborate hive-like system. Little else is known about them. It is believed that the Hist were the first inhabitants of Nirn and that they created the Argonians. Today, many believe that the two species share a symbiotic relationship. The sap of the Hist tree when consumed gives an Argonian hatchling its soul, and when that Argonian dies, their soul travels back to its Hist tree and is stored until another hatchling drinks that Hist and is given its soul, creating a cycle of rebirth. Whether this is fact or just beliefs associated with the worship of the Hist is unknown. 
Hist priests have a special bond with these trees, they are able to talk to the Hist and souls kept within each tree to learn the secrets of the past. They are also able to draw upon the tree for magicka.

{Penitus_Oculatus_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\n\nThe Penitus Oculatus was formed as the Emperor's espionage and bodyguard organization, presumably soon after the Oblivion Crisis, as the Blades abandoned their role as Imperial bodyguards with the death of the Dragonborn Emperors. The Penitus Oculatus gained greater duties and importance following the Great War and the dissolution of the Blades dictated by the White-Gold Concordat. They also double as Imperial assassins. The organization requires its members to have sharp senses and a willingness to kill. Their recruitment process is oftentimes a brutal one, as displayed by Inspector Colin's task to assassinate an old man. The purpose of such a recruitment process was to weed out those unwilling to actually go through with murder and other usually immoral tasks. Penitus Oculatus roughly translates to "The Inner Eyes" or, literally, to "Inwardly having Eyes". Members of the Penitus Oculatus are known as inspectors, although they are colloquially referred to as "spectres".

Since only a few people can withstand prolonged exposure to this strange city, the basis of the population is Bosmer, Orcs and Imga. Since the beginning of the Fourth era, during the reign of the Aldmeri Dominion, the city ceased to bear the status of the capital. Orcs living in Falinesti among Bosmer soon learn rare for this people skills of archery.

There is credible information that suggests that Lizandas, King of Daggerfall, was killed as a result of machinations involving the Knights of the Rose.

{Knights_of_the_Rose_descr_short}Valiant riders and loyal defenders of the Kingdom of Wayrest.

Today, the clans of Black Marsh do not have anything that resembles a standing army, but it is worth noting that if their home country faces any danger, Argonians will come to her defense with nearly unmatched fury. This may be due to their subordination by the Hist which may order the Argonians to protect their native swamps until the last drop of blood.
Men-at-Arms wear distinctive armor and are made up of some of the better warriors of each Clan. Undoubtedly, their advantages include their high morale and better organization than that of other Black Marsh forces.

{Argonian_Men_At_Arms_descr_short}Skilled Clan warriors, armed with shields and swords.

Shadowscale Archers prefer to execute their targets from a distance; their bows and arrows feel like extensions of their own bodies after years of practice.

{Hlaalu_Men_At_Arms}Hombres de armas hlaalu

{Hlaalu_Men_At_Arms_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain bonus: Barren\n\nGreat House Hlaalu is currently the most influential off all the Great Houses. One of its own, Hlaalu Helseth, is by the grace of the Empire king of whole Morrowind. Despite the fact that Hlaalu are the youngest of Houses, they managed to completely monopolize external and internal trade lines, as well as securing their position at court. As House Hlaalu's territory borders that of Cyrodiil and though they are not a warrior house, the Hlaalu quickly embraced the Empire soon after they arrived in Morrowind. House Hlaalu welcomes Imperial Law and the Legions, and the freedom of trade and religion. Most Hlaalu are bribable, either with gold or with other favours, with their leaders as no exception. House Hlaalu retainers are also masters of thievery, sneaking, lock-picking, blackmailing and backstabbing, both literal and metaphorical. As soon as Hlaalu reached for lands of the other Houses, they clashed with them, and found themselves in need of creating more professional troops. Typical Armun-Ra Bonemold armor of native Hlaalu design is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Hlaalu design allows its wearer to simultaneously protect himself, and retain high flexibility of movement.

{Hlaalu_Men_At_Arms_descr_short}Soldiers clad in lighter version of bonemold armor of Armun-Ra native design, armed with swords and shields.

Their red armor is inspired by native House Indoril design, with a little bit of Helseth’s personal preferences. They are concentrated in Mournhold, capital of Morrowind. They are trained soldiers protecting lives of the royal family. Royal Guard equally accepts members of all races, making it a very flexible, elite force. They are armed with silver, enchanted swords and large towershields.

{Kings_Guard_descr_short}Highly trained royal warriors clad in iron, red armor and equipped with long, silver swords and towershields.

{Hlaalu_Cavalry}Caballería hlaalu

{Hlaalu_Cavalry_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 90%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nSecondary Attack: 10\nTerrain bonus: Barren\nTerrain malus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\n\nGreat House Hlaalu is currently the most influential off all the Great Houses. One of its own, Hlaalu Helseth, is by the grace of the Empire king of whole Morrowind. Despite the fact that Hlaalu are the youngest of Houses, they managed to completely monopolize external and internal trade lines, as well as securing their position at court. As House Hlaalu's territory borders that of Cyrodiil and though they are not a warrior house, the Hlaalu quickly embraced the Empire soon after they arrived in Morrowind. House Hlaalu welcomes Imperial Law and the Legions, and the freedom of trade and religion. Most Hlaalu are bribable, either with gold or with other favours, with their leaders as no exception. House Hlaalu retainers are also masters of thievery, sneaking, lock-picking, blackmailing and backstabbing, both literal and metaphorical. As soon as Hlaalu reached for lands of the other Houses, they clashed with them, and found themselves in need of creating more professional troops. Typical Armun-Ra Bonemold armor of native Hlaalu design is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Hlaalu design allows its wearer to simultaneously protect himself, and retain high flexibility of movement.

{Hlaalu_Cavalry_descr_short}Soldiers clad in lighter version of bonemold armor of Armun-Ra native design, armed with swords and shields. They fight from the backs of mighty warguars.

¬----------------

{Telvanni_Wizards_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 140 meters\nMissiles: 7\nArmour Piercing Missiles\n\nGreat House Telvanni consists mainly of ungodly ambitious magicians who, for the sake of climbing in House’s deadly hierarchy, do not hesitate from most brutal and immortal methods of fighting with their rivals. Telvanni could not care less for a life of ordinary subject, division, dark ways of thinking -- these are one of the most positive aspects of the House Telvanni... However, when some external enemy is threatening even smallest member of the House, Telvanni can unite and quickly serve harsh, painful justice. Wizard-lords command devastating spells that can decimate whole armies in seconds, and their rage knows no limits -- these are Telvanni’s weapons, only accompanied by more conventional swords and staves.

{Dres_Wizards}Magos dres

{Dres_Wizards_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 100%\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 140 meters\nMissiles: 5\nArmour Piercing Missiles\n\nMages from the Great House of Dres have almost as much magical ability as the wizard-lords of the House Telvanni, but they trade off raw damage for larger area of effects, allowing for easier decimation of massed argonian slaves in revolt. Dres magicians are especially known from their eclectic approach to the arcane schools, melding spells usually thought to belong to Alteration with more traditional Destruction curses. Mages of House Dres are force to be reckoned with -- their fire magicks, used to burn large swaths of impenetrable Black Marsh, are especially dangerous...

{Dres_Wizards_descr_short}True masters of magic arts, wielding great powers of the arcane.

{Velothi_Caster}Místicos velothi

{Velothi_Caster_descr}Mages from the Great Houses of Hlaalu and Redoran in no way can be compared with wizard-lords of the House Telvanni, or even the crafty Magicians of House Dres, but they still wield considerable and terrible powers. Velothi Mystics in terms of power level are more comparable to Hist Priests or Winterhold Mages, as they get their powers not from arcane study, but from spiritual devotion to the tribunal. The spirit of Sotha Sil does his best to grant them their powers. Even though their powers are divinely given, due to the Tribunal's distrust of magic users, due to their extensive history with witches and witch hunting, they do not employ mystics or spellweavers of any kind, unless they are registered witch hunters.

{Velothi_Caster_descr_short}True masters of magic arts, wielding great powers of the arcane.

{Dres_Men_At_Arms}Hombres de armas dres

{Dres_Men_At_Arms_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest\n\nGreat House Dres -- one of the Dunmeri Great Houses. Dres own territories territory in the southern part of Morrowind, where the fertile plains of Deshaan turn into rivers and swamps of Argonia. They revere the Temple -- mostly -- and are very hostile towards culture and customs of the Empire. During the invasion of the Imperial Legions on Morrowind, at the beginning of Third Era, House Dres supported the initiative of the House Indoril -- fight to the death, fight at all costs. It was in this time that Dres troops became famous because of their guerrilla tactics and very flexible strategy. Typical Dres Bonemold armor is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Dres design allows its wearer to simultaneously protect himself, and retain high flexibility of movement, even in a very hostile environment of the Black Marsh.

{Dres_Men_At_Arms_descr_short}Soldiers clad in lighter version of bonemold armor of Dres native design, armed with swords and shields.

{Redoran_Swordsmen}Espadachines redoran

{Redoran_Swordsmen_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\n\nMembers of the Great House Redoran are traditionalists who fulfill their oaths of piety and honor with devotion to both their families and their clans. Serving the Tribunes and ancestral worship are also an important part of Redoran’s life, at home and in public. As one of the five Great Houses, Redoran actively influence great politics of Morrowind, albeit -- with its devotion to the Old Ways -- staying a little bit behind ones like Hlaalu or Telvanni. War is one of the main aspects of the Redoran -- their society is highly militarized. Soldiers of House Redoran wear native, traditional Gah-Julan Bonemold armor. The armor is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Redoran design -- tested in thousands of battles, since this Great House is House of warriors -- allows its wearer to simultaneously protect himself, and retain high flexibility of movement, even during deadly ashstorms of Vvardenfell.

{Redoran_Swordsmen_descr_short}Disciplined soldiers clad in bonemold armor of Gah-Julan native design, armed with longswords and large shields.

As one of the five Great Houses, Redoran actively influence great politics of Morrowind, albeit -- with its devotion to the Old Ways -- staying a little bit behind ones like Hlaalu or Telvanni. Defenders of Faith is a non-short of legendary military commando, very experienced in the field and operating outside normal chain of command. They specialize in waging sacred war against enemies of the Tribunal faith -- heretics, apostates, heathens and infidels. They do wear Gah-Julan Bonemold native armor as a typical outfit of a soldier belonging to the Great House Redoran. The armor is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Defenders of Faith are also armed with powerful, enchanted, silver halberds.

{Redoran_Women_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\n\nMembers of the Great House Redoran are traditionalists who fulfill their oaths of piety and honor with devotion to both their families and their clans. Serving the Tribunes and ancestral worship are also an important part of Redoran’s life, at home and in public. As one of the five Great Houses, Redoran actively influence great politics of Morrowind, albeit -- with its devotion to the Old Ways -- staying a little bit behind ones like Hlaalu or Telvanni. War is one of the main aspects of the Redoran -- their society is highly militarized. Men and women are equal in this subject, it’s not uncommon that wives and daughters take up arms just like their husbands and fathers. When going like soldiers, Redoran women also wear native, traditional Gah-Julan Bonemold armor. It is a typical outfit of a soldier belonging to the Great House Redoran. The armor is composed of bones which are artificially shaped and assembled before being fixed with resin glues. In addition, this particular set is reinforced with tough netch skin and dark iron plates. Redoran design -- tested in thousands of battles, since this Great House is House of warriors -- allows its wearer to simultaneously protect himself, and retain high flexibility of movement, even during deadly ashstorms of Vvardenfell.

Full Legion stations are maintained mainly in most hazardous areas of an Empire, like in ash-covered island of Vvardenfell in Morrowind, or on the dangerous border between Transniben region of Cyrodiil and Black Marsh. \n  Those deployed as ‘Imperial Watch’ act in a policing capacity, supplementing the forces of locally appointed lords in the guarding of towns, supply lines, fortifications, and roads.  Often made up of recent recruits, as well as those who have yet to experience battle; Watchmen take up important duties that keep the more experienced soldiers of the Empire where they are needed.  Equipped in traditional imperial garments and armor, as well as wielding effective Colovian longbows, these Imperial Watchmen fight from afar, acting as a necessary skirmishing force, to the bane of those who upset the Emperor's peace.

{Argonian_Militia_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Poor\nMovement Speed: 130%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nIn 1E 2811, Cyrodillic Imperials clashed with and destroyed the armies of Black Marsh. Argonia saw little military victory until the later years of the Third Empire when, during the Arnesian War, it was able to seize Dunmer territories in the north. Black Marsh was then annexed into the Third Empire through diplomacy rather than military force.  Today, the clans of Black Marsh do not have anything that resembles a standing army, but it is worth noting that if their home country faces any danger, Argonians will come to her defense with nearly unmatched fury. This may be due to their subordination by the Hist which may order the Argonians to protect their native swamps until the last drop of blood.  These troops are essentially militias comprising ordinary citizens of the clans of Black Marsh. They wear only the clothes on their backs, but have natural protection - their scales. Each Argonian makes the wooden spear and shield that he wields. Their weak defense is compensated by their tendency towards secrecy: their movement is of a swift and silent nature.

{High_Ordinators}Altos ordenantes

{High_Ordinators_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain malus: Shrubs\n\nOrdinators, also called “Order of Ordinators”, are a special military order of sacred provenience, composed of elite warriors devoted to the Tribunal Temple as serving exclusively under its orders. History of the Ordinators is connected with the history of the Temple itself, more precisely with an individual today known as St. Olms. Ordinator ranks are strictly limited to Dunmer, and only those Dunmer who hail from within the province of Morrowind. Ordinators are easily recognizable by their unique Indoril Armor which are golden and heavily polished. They are divided into four distinct orders. The Order of the Watch provides guards for temples and shrines, and additionally act as town guards in Temple-owned settlements such as Vivec City. They are responsible with enforcing Temple law, along with Imperial law following the Armistice. The Order of War fights the enemies of the Temple, with most War Ordinators traditionally stationed near Red Mountain to combat the forces of the Sixth House. The Order of the Inquisition identifies and suppresses heresy among priests and laymen within the Temple. The Order of Doctrine and Ordination are militant scholars. Among other Ordinators these called the High Ordinators are the most dangerous ones -- they are selected from best among the best of normal members of the Order, serving in capital of Morrowind, holy city of Almalexia.

{High_Ordinators_descr_short}Highly devoted and disciplined warriors of the Tribunal Temple, clad in famous, golden armor and armed with ebony swords.

{Psijics}Psijics

{Psijics_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 100%\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 180 meters\nMissiles: 9\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Barren, Forest, Snow\n\n“In Mundus, conflict and disparity are what bring change, and change is the most sacred of the Eleven Forces. Change is the force without focus or origin.” -- The Monomyth, Oegnithr, Taheritae, Order of PSJJJJ\n\n The Psijic Order or PSJJJJ is the oldest monastic group of Tamriel, devoted to the study and practice of Mysticism, which they call the "Old Ways" or "Elder Way". They make their home on the Isle of Artaeum in the Summerset archipelago. The Order was founded during the early settlement of the Summerset Isle by the Aldmer. During this time, there was a movement to change the religion of Aldmeri society: instead of pure, ecumenical ancestor worship, the Aldmer of Summerset began worshipping only the ancestors claimed by the elite of the Aldmeri society, from which the traditional Aldmeri pantheon of Aedra emerged. A group of dissident Aldmer Elders rejected this change and left Summerset to settle on the nearby island of Artaeum. The Order is first mentioned in writing in the year 1E 20 by the Breton sage Voernet, who traveled to Artaeum to meet with the Psijic Ritemaster, Iachesis. In these early days, Psijics were described as counselors to royalty and teachers of the "Elder Way", a philosophy of meditation and study related to the magic of Mysticism. They also kept the entrance to the Dreaming Cave, a portal to Oblivion. Sotha Sil is believed to have been a friend of the Psijics, possibly a member himself, and to have used this portal to speak with the Daedric Princes.

{Psijics_descr_short}Legendary monks from the island Artaeum, archmasters of arcane arts.

{Mehrunes_Elephant}Mehrunes Dagon

{Mehrunes_Elephant_descr}"When I will walk the earth again, the Faithful among you shall receive your reward: to be set above all other mortals forever. As for the rest: the weak shall be winnowed: the timid shall be cast down: the mighty shall tremble at my feet and pray for pardon." When in the mortal realm, Mehrunes Dagon appeared as a gigantic demonic creature with red skin, yellow eyes and four arms, each one armed with a deadly weapon, one such arm had metal claws protruding from its fist. Six horns adorned Dagon's bald skull and his pointed ears were pierced with large gold hoops. His entire body was scarred and tattooed with ritualistic markings. Sharp fangs lined his jaw, and he wore an armoured neck brace around his shoulders. His hands and feet were clawed and he frequently used them in battle to stomp on and crush his enemies.  His spheres are destruction, change, revolution, energy, and ambition. Mehrunes Dagon is the foe of all mortal races, and has attempted to conquer the physical world of Nirn many times. The common reasoning behind his many plots is his belief that Tamriel is in fact a plane of Oblivion which is rightly his. The driving force behind the Oblivion Crisis, he plotted to destroy the Septim bloodline and open gates to Oblivion throughout Cyrodiil to launch an invasion.

In peacetime -- rare commodity in Tamriel -- Legions work in the provinces, patrol the streets and imperial roads, guard merchants, perform administrative work, and so on. Full Legions station mainly in most hazardous areas of an Empire, like in ash-covered island of Vvardenfell in Morrowind, or on the dangerous border between Transniben region of Cyrodiil and Black Marsh. In times of conflict -- and Third Empire is almost always at war, bigger or smaller -- the Legions are fully mobilized and deployed to the field. \n\n Light-armoured soldiers are standard troops of the mighty Imperial Legion. They move quickly on foot and are very mobile in battle, for that they exchange protection -- Light Legionnaires wear only lightest of armours. They are armed with swords and shields and are -- usually -- trained to fight in large formations, but they are neither the bravest nor most experienced troops.

{Draugr_Infantry}Draugrs

{Draugr_Infantry_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\n\nDraugr are a special kind of undead. The Draugr are a type of "barely living" men of Atmoran and Nordic origin typically found in the province of Skyrim. They are, along with Dragon Priests, classified as undead, despite carrying a "whisper of life," as some scholars theorize.  This feature is believed to be related to their relation to the Dragon Cult and its priests, to whom the Draugr act as immortal servants. The Skaal seem to believe that the Draugr were cursed with undeath by the "All-Maker", due to having feasted on their comrades after becoming trapped in the island of Solstheim.  Others believe that they were cursed to never die by the Dragons, after man betrayed them in the Dragon War.  Unlike other forms of the undead, draugrs tend to be harder to control.  They're characterized by the presence of withered flesh and blue, glowing eyes.  

{Draugr_Infantry_descr_short}Barely-living undead mainly of Nordic origin.

Others believe that they were cursed to never die by the Dragons, after man betrayed them in the Dragon War.  Unlike other forms of the undead, draugrs tend to be harder to control.  They're characterized by the presence of withered flesh and blue, glowing eyes.  

{Merc_Draugr_Spearmen_descr_short}Barely-living undead mainly of Nordic origin.

{Ashlanders_Merc}Cenicios

{Ashlanders_Merc_descr}Ashlanders -- Dunmeri nomadic tribes with a strong sense of pride and tradition. They primarily live in the Ashlands, never staying in one spot for too long before moving onto the next. Each tribe is led by a Chieftain, but a great deal of respect is given to the shaman of the tribe who serves as a source of information and prophecy. Ashlanders adhere to ancient traditions and are opposed to the "cult of the Tribunal", they remember that it was Nerevar who killed Dagoth Ur and was later betrayed by the Tribunal. Some Ashlanders attack trade caravans that pass through the Ashlands, although larger tribes tend to be much more peaceful while still maintaining a cold demeanor to strangers. Each Ashlander will fight to the death to protect their tribe and beliefs, they prefer hit-and-run tactics using their faithful mounts, the guar.

{Ashlanders_Merc_descr_short}Ashlander hunters riding on guars.

{Sotha_Sil_Custom_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nUnique Unit\nGeneral's Unit\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 110 meters\nMissiles: 4\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nGunpowder Unit\n\nSotha Sil, also called Lord Seht, was one of the members of the Tribunal, the divine rulers of Morrowind. For his feats, Sotha Sil was called an architect of time, a binder of Oblivion, and the "Mystery of Morrowind." He is known by many other titles, such as the Clockwork God, Father of Mysteries, the Inspiration of Craft and Sorcery, the Light of Knowledge, Magus, Magician, Sorcerer, Tinkerer, the "si" in Almsivi. He was said to be reshaping the world from his hidden Clockwork City. Sotha Sil the Magus was one of the three God-like Kings of Morrowind, a Pillar of the Tribunal Temple, and the patron of artificers and wizards. Sotha Sil was the mightiest wizard and the wisest councilor of the First Council, and also spent time training the Psijic Order on Artaeum. He was the companion and teacher of Vivec and Nerevar. According to the Tribunal Temple Cult, Sotha Sil was the Light of Knowledge and the Inspiration of Craft and Sorcery, and Azura was the Anticipation of Sotha Sil.

{Sotha_Sil_Custom_descr_short}God of Knowledge

{Gloom_Wraiths}Espectros sombríos

{Gloom_Wraiths_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 80%\nFree Upkeep Unit\n\nA Wraith is essentially a more powerful Ghost. Most of their features are hidden by a large, tattered black cloak, showing only their face and hands. The skin of a Wraith is a pale, dirty green, and their face is usually contorted in a terrifying scream. Since no feet emerge from the bottom of the cloak and they move by hovering, it can be safe to assume that their body structure is the same as a Ghost - a head, a torso, and two arms. Gloom Wraiths also seem to have a tiara-like structure of spikes upon their forehead like a crown with a beard, and Faded Wraiths are less visible and ghost-like than an ordinary Wraith. Unlike Ghosts, Wraiths appear to be fully or at least slightly solid, as they are able to wield weapons. 

{Gloom_Wraiths_descr_short}Summoned wraiths of long-dead people.

{Mehrunes_Dagon}Mehrunes Dagon

{Mehrunes_Dagon_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 90%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Barren, Snow\n\nLocked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 90%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Barren, Snow\n\n"When I will walk the earth again, the Faithful among you shall receive your reward: to be set above all other mortals forever. As for the rest: the weak shall be winnowed: the timid shall be cast down: the mighty shall tremble at my feet and pray for pardon." When in the mortal realm, Mehrunes Dagon appeared as a gigantic demonic creature with red skin, yellow eyes and four arms, each one armed with a deadly weapon, one such arm had metal claws protruding from its fist. Six horns adorned Dagon's bald skull and his pointed ears were pierced with large gold hoops. His entire body was scarred and tattooed with ritualistic markings. Sharp fangs lined his jaw, and he wore an armoured neck brace around his shoulders. His hands and feet were clawed and he frequently used them in battle to stomp on and crush his enemies.  His spheres are destruction, change, revolution, energy, and ambition. Mehrunes Dagon is the foe of all mortal races, and has attempted to conquer the physical world of Nirn many times. The common reasoning behind his many plots is his belief that Tamriel is in fact a plane of Oblivion which is rightly his. The driving force behind the Oblivion Crisis, he plotted to destroy the Septim bloodline and open gates to Oblivion throughout Cyrodiil to launch an invasion.

{Mehrunes_Dagon_descr_short}Mehrunes Dagon himself, intent on leaving destruction wherever he goes.

With having to defend against constant raids from the Reach, skirmishing with the Redguards, and trade in the Illiac Bay, these Bretons wear coats of purple as a sign of wealth, but also wear armor that is lighter so as to fight more agile and swifter to adapt to their foes..

{Dragon}Dragones
{Dragon_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 300%\n\nDragons (Dovah, or Dov in plural) are a reptilian race from Akavir that were once widespread throughout Tamriel. Although seeming rather beast-like, they are profoundly intelligent, capable of speech, written language, and comprehension of other languages. Their spoken language can be used to cast powerful magic known as Thu'um or dragon shouts. Thought to have gone extinct during the Merithic Era after the Great Dragon Civil War. But have returned to seek revenge and to bring about the End.
{Dragon_descr_short}Huge reptilian beasts that can breathe fire or frost, and wield the powers of the Voice

He is noted as the chief enemy of Pelinal Whitestrake. Called "the unfeathered" because of his wings, he was an immortal sorcerer, favored of Meridia, and the son of an Et'ada, who is noted as being a god of a previous kalpa.

{Umaril_descr_short}The great Ayleid sorcerer-king guarded by powerful Aurorians.

{Aurorians}Auroranos

{Aurorians_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 100%\n\n The Auroran is a type of lesser Daedra in the service of the Daedric Princess Meridia and residing in her Oblivion plane - the Colored Rooms. These are humanoid Daedra, in appearance resembling an elven-style armored person, or rather, a mer, given their height (more than two meters in height). Most likely, the outer shell is the armor, since it does not completely hide the body (there is a small gap between the helmet and the cuirass). It was they who were called to serve the Ayleids during their reign.

{Aurorians_descr_short}Lesser Daedra in the service of the Daedric Princess Meridia, strong and dreadful warriors.

{Kotn_Sergeants}Sargentos de los nueve

{Kotn_Sergeants_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nFree Upkeep Unit\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\n The Order of the Knights of the Nine was founded in 3E 111 with the ambitious intent of recovering the lost Relics of the Divine Crusader, Pelinal Whitestrake, who was killed while fighting the forces of the Ayleid king, Umaril the Unfeathered. The order was founded by Sir Amiel Lannus, a hero of the War of the Isle. He named his order the Knights of the Nine after the Nine Divines, and set up base at the Priory of the Nine in the West Weald. Knights are most holy of all orders of the Nine Divines religion, devoted to worship of the pantheon as a whole. They are elite warriors, one of the best in all of Tamriel.

{Kotn_Sergeants_descr_short}Sergeants of the Nine are a simple men who gave their lives in service of the Order of the Nine.
